# OpenRune migration continuation ledger

Active program; not complete. Branch: agent2/openrune-content. Agent 2 owns CLI commits/pushes and issue evidence. Approved elevated Git execution as backup with exact per-command safe.directory remains required. Preserve all unstaged packaging differences and ignored resources.

## Inventory boundary

OPENRUNE_CONTENT_INVENTORY.csv contains 3,508 discovered source/configuration file records from content, api, engine, server and or-cache. These are a discovery denominator, NOT a verified gameplay-module denominator or completeness percentage. Build/generated/editor directories are excluded. Registration names are lexical candidates, not proof of reachable behavior. Other root configuration, pack inputs/assets and indirectly loaded content still require reconciliation. Most records remain NOT_ASSESSED. No reference implementation or assets are copied into the repository.

Source identity: local OpenRune archive configured for revision 241, no upstream Git commit. File SHA256 is recorded per row. Root ISC and file-level exceptions require per-file clearance before translation. Current work independently adapts behavior to existing Rust architecture; no Kotlin source expression was translated.

## Batch 001: queued smithing requirements

Selected SmeltingScript.kt SHA256 13b6dd208704723eb134ef19440e60a34a5c075f23bb4d8138108350907a011c and AnvilSmithingScript.kt SHA256 6ae9ca5103c42329d5abc98d10880063eda644c9b7a00c572f82a1e351b83867. Actual startup registers furnace/category use, anvil operations and production queues. OpenRune rechecks current requirements for queued jobs. Local PG SmithingActions.continueJob checks current inputs but retains admission-only level/hammer checks. Rust now carries required level and non-consumed inventory tools into its existing Request and checks them before each completion's RNG, ingredient mutation or XP. Anvil binds its existing hammer; furnace binds its existing recipe level. No new IDs, timers or transaction engine.

This deliberately tightens local-PG behavior. It does not claim Kotlin parity for the new requirement boundary. Inventory affordability remains compared to 12,500 cached Kotlin vectors. Existing ordinary-iron impurity and XP atomicity work, previously uncommitted, is included in this production batch with its existing tests.

Wiki support: existing #79 normal-furnace level/recipe requirements and #82 Iron bar revision15315108; Hammer article https://oldschool.runescape.wiki/w/Hammer supports anvil hammer requirement and Imcando substitution. This run's live Hammer retrieval had no pinned revision; do not claim historical revision attestation. Existing 239.1 symbol-based level/hammer binding is retained. No Imcando/equipped-tool support added. Exact live revalidation timing is the selected server safety policy, not a claimed Wiki tick quote.

- New regression covers tool removal and level loss before first and repeated reward; no extra material/XP or lingering queue.
- Native client, target-scene attestation, same-save relogin and multiplayer: NOT TESTED for this batch. #79/#82 remain open. No full smelting/anvil acceptance claim.

## Pineapple slicing overflow evidence (2026-10-16 UTC; not implemented)

Bounded read-only local Wiki follow-up: Pineapple revision15302325, wikitext SHA256 `e3138bad10c668e4eef898021828079c908d79d3b0a85b2c1d05e77957e6231b`, explicitly states that rings drop to the ground when inventory space is insufficient. Pineapple ring revision15184663 (`988e52cf5861e02cb2ea8acb4e9ec1b7e47094a18550445270b84bc4145a2b8c`) specifies four rings per ordinary pineapple, knife retained, members, two ticks. Chunks revision15183792 (`61870cc9c8ad20407c819b47169e16db79deba6c79c1a94a17bf5a8d68c33f86`) specifies the separate one-chunk Dice route. Tenti variants remain excluded.

Therefore a prospective slicing implementation must not silently reuse the one-for-one grinding transaction or claim full Wiki acceptance with a full-inventory refusal. Reuse the existing inventory and ground-object primitives, but establish the exact partial inventory/overflow split, ground ownership and expiry, capacity-failure atomicity, menu selection and two-clock timing first. Recipe entries contain no explicit skill/XP fields; missing fields remain distinct from an executed zero-XP observation. No slicing source/runtime changes or acceptance claim in this review. Pineapple topping acceptance is unaffected.

## Herb transaction and profile preparation (2026-10-16 UTC; uncompiled)

Added herb_cleaning transaction component using existing Inventory delete/insert and skills::advance. It stages inventory and stats together, rechecks supplied current membership/quest eligibility and boosted level, rejects stale selected slots without substitute herbs, keeps exact slots in a full bag and accepts fine-XP recipe values25/38. Four authored tests cover fractional5x rewards/replay, member/quest/level/refused-credit/invalid-rate rollback, stale/notes/count/vars and boosted marrentill with an existing fractional remainder. A separate prepared content profile builder resolves target Names and requires ordinary member definitions, unique inputs/outputs and exact Clean operations; its authored malformed-definition test covers eight rejection categories.

NO live handler, automatic cleaning scheduler, quest binding or startup call was added. Timing/mixed-herb policy remains unresolved. This is UNCOMPILED preparation, not functional herb acceptance. Source is independently authored from pinned Wiki values and existing Rust transaction APIs; no OpenRune expression copied. Pinned native pie archive7C3952D6 predates these source additions and remains immutable.

Additional target opcode16 decode confirms all four herb items are members items; prior item report preserved. Wiki Guam leaf15183585 and Marrentill15183543 explicitly mark Clean herb recipes boostable at3/5 and2.5/3.8XP. Herblore15347160 requires Druidic Ritual completion for training. Automatic hourly estimates were not converted into a claimed exact cadence.

## Unfinished-potion shared runtime registration (2026-10-16 UTC)

The fifteen validated ordinary water profiles now route held Use in either selected order into the existing single-choice Make-X dialog and weak scheduler. Initial exact slots are retained; repetitions select only remaining inputs for that chosen recipe. Each commit rechecks current membership, exact Druidic Ritual completion, boosted Herblore level, selected item count/vars and atomic credit. No XP reward. Existing held-action/modal/movement/logout cancellation remains authoritative; no parallel production engine.

Wiki per-output timing interpretation is recorded in work/openrune-unfinished-potion-timing-semantics-001.json: all fifteen recipes ticks1, no ticksnote override; Template:Recipe/doc15110729 and Module:Recipe15224963 clarify output cadence. This independently selects one server clock between queue and commit/repeats, rather than OpenRune weak1/repeat4 or local-added Kotlin delay3. No compiled original-Kotlin unfinished-potion parity claim.

## Antidote and blood-base finished recipes (2026-10-16)

Expanded25to29production profiles using the existing exact-pair scheduler and atomic inventory/fineXP transaction. Antidote+ and Antidote++ produce their native four-dose items; Bastion and Battlemage produce three-dose items. Only unfinished-plus-secondary recipes are implemented; combined three-input recipes and drinking effects are separate.

## Divine potion runtime: corrected dust and fractional XP (native pending)

Registered all 32 dose recipes across eight OpenRune divine families through the existing dose-upgrade Make and scheduler path. Each input dose consumes one ordinary crystal dust and awards 0.5 XP, with a two-tick cadence. The recipes enforce the goggles exclusion internally; a caller cannot accidentally enable secondary saving. Ordinary chemistry remains eligible below four input doses, with no extra ingredient cost or XP. Each family has its own next-dose map.

The donor uses sote_crystal_dust (23804), which independently decodes as nonstackable quest dust with Destroy and an Arianwyn replacement message. The pinned Wiki identifies ordinary Crystal dust as23964 and the quest variant as23804. Correct binding: prif_crystal_shard_crushed23964. All65 ordinary input/output/dust definitions were decoded and verified. The donor also supplies5XP per dose; the eight pinned Wiki recipe pages specify0.5XP. These discrepancies were corrected, not ported. Crystal-shard acquisition and crushing have a separate Song of the Elves requirement and are outside this potion-production implementation.

## Super combat transaction foundation (runtime and native pending)

Added an atomic four-input transaction using existing Inventory and skills::advance. Three four-dose potions plus either clean Torstol269 or Torstol potion(unf)111 produce Super combat potion(4)12695 at boosted Herblore90,150baseXP. Goggles can retain only Torstol; this always-four-dose output has no chemistry roll. Inputs, definitions, gates and both potential inventories validate before RNG and publication. Runtime selection, Make routing, two-tick scheduling, native acceptance and persistence remain pending. No full parity claim.

OpenRune Herblore.kt lines410-420 instead specifies three-dose ingredients/output and omits the unfinished alternative. The selected Wiki recipe explicitly requires four-dose ingredients/output and both Torstol forms; this mismatch is preserved rather than inherited. Six target item definitions were independently decoded in work/openrune-supercombat-cache-evidence-001.json.

## Super combat shared runtime integration (native pending)

Registered both independently decoded Torstol recipes through the existing held-use/Make interface and world scheduler. Torstol may be selected against any of the three four-dose bases in either order. The selected herb/potion slots are retained; the other two bases are resolved from the live inventory. Every input is checked again at commit; subsequent jobs resolve the next complete set. Movement, logout, modal, live member/quest/level and stale ingredient conditions cancel or reject without partial mutation. Production commits in two ticks and awards1500base fineXP, with the existing personal rate applied. No new inventory, XP, tick or persistence engine.

## Compatible dose-upgrade extension: anti-venom and extended antifire (native pending)

Added eight Wiki-backed recipes through the existing OpenRune-informed dose transaction/Make/scheduler. Anti-venom uses five Zulrah scales per input dose,30XP/dose,Herblore87; extended antifire uses one lava shard per input dose,27.5XP/dose,Herblore84. Both preserve one-to-four input doses, two-tick cadence, goggles and eligible chemistry. No new engine. These are explicitly Wiki-supported coverage extensions, not a claim that the inspected donor table contained correct matching rows.

Independent decoding of18target definitions confirms symbol2antidragon means Extended antifire11951/11953/11955/11957. The donor row labelled super antifire instead uses lantadyme vial plus crushed bones and3dose2antidragon; that row is not silently accepted as correct. True super antifire and its learned Primula/DragonSlayerII gate remain separate. Candidate varbit6140 super_antifire_unlocked was found, but its writer/value semantics are not established and no gate is fabricated. Forgotten brew4has145XP versus36perdose for lower doses; it was not forced into a linear award formula.

- Extended antifire: revision15185448, raw SHA256 `635b491098364af6cc80f8a80f75b2166aa860eec3635eda1b21362a4d6fa7e8`.

## Acceptance archive source identities

Each build compares its complete397(or394)source-file hash map before and after compilation, then copies the binary and source manifest into an immutable archive. These manifests describe the actual preserved working tree, including pre-existing changes; a Git checkpoint alone is not substituted for the executable/source evidence. Current native sessions use pinned paths and do not read a later rebuilt target executable.

- `divine-native-001`: executable SHA256`29BB4FD3587E30689BCAFA18C2DEF348ACC58653E4AB6C81FEF489D400A311FD`,394source files, source-hashes.json SHA256`5cb5aa5969c34ba6ca0857dfe48e3a9a5f27a65450702d422f698dbb8780acb2`.
- `combatmixing-native-001`: executable SHA256`5EF5A3FD7BFB560C25C6AFBB7461FD71EB30764B95CB216727FF6A091150C4D9`,397source files, source-hashes.json SHA256`52a315d6141d36aa7a97b9dd2ded9eb1e50f17ec72ced5198433a7412cec847e`.

## Native gameplay combatmixing acceptance (2026-10-17 UTC)

Six native four-input super combat groups: clean and unfinished Torstol, all three selected base types and alternating order. Exact six four-dose outputs,45000fineXP at5x, two-tick commits, selected slots, unrelated state and same-save no replay. Goggles native proc, acquisition and drinking remain separate.

All listed runs require client exit0, fresh durable logout, clean owned exit0 and passing independent state/trace assessment. Same-save predecessor identity is preserved in pack-run.json; relogin runs assert no replay. This does not establish rendered appearance, multiplayer, full Cooking/Herblore parity or unrelated recipes. No issue closure.

## Forgotten brew production: explicit dose awards (native pending)

Registered four Forgotten brew upgrades through the existing selected-stack transaction, Make dialog, scheduler and per-family chemistry map. Wiki revision 15191164, wikitext SHA256 `6d8bfc03fb71e599e9996353d99a5a7eafb727f3d501e1d734062d8ee5154bc1`, requires Herblore91 (boostable),20 ancient essence per input dose,three ticks and explicit36/72/108/145XP. Four doses deliberately award145 rather than144. Recipe.base_fine_xp() supplies both advancement and transition trace; existing linear recipes retain their original awards through None overrides. Invalid zero total awards refuse before debit/RNG. Goggles retain the whole required stack on a single proc; chemistry continues to charge input-dose costs/XP.

Independent target decoding verified nine definitions: ancient essence27616, Ancient brew26346/26344/26342/26340 and Forgotten brew27638/27635/27632/27629; all normal members definitions and correct stackability/Drink operations. Private decode evidence SHA256 `f7f27ba17889cf030ebce037a12e49d228dacda8aba979dbbe68bc8cac157d9c`. This is a Wiki-supported extension; donor-wide parity and drinking effects are separate.

## Ancient brew production dependency: selected stack support (native pending)

Wiki Ancient brew revision15342079, raw SHA256 `19f243f4203bb7869f71ecbddcf817fee2dd185a5eaeca7efd71e53b51686120`: one Nihil dust plus unfinished dwarf weed potion produces Ancient brew(3),Herblore85(boostable),190XP,two ticks. Target decoding verifies Nihil dust26368 is stackable; dwarf weed unfinished109 and Ancient brew26342/26340 are ordinary members items with correct operations. Ancient brew now joins the existing finished-potion Make/scheduler and a separately attested27th ordinary chemistry3-to4dose mapping.

Ordinary finished-potion transactions accept positive-count secondaries only when the immutable catalog declares that secondary stackable; unfinished vials and nonstackable secondaries still require exactly one. Strict selected-slot atomic debit consumes one dust, retains the rest and preserves unrelated slots/XP. Automatic repeats pin stackable secondaries to the original selected slot and refuse if it moves/disappears; individual secondaries retain existing first-valid selection. No new inventory or gameplay engine. Nihil shard grinding,acquisition and drinking remain separate.

Registered this specific stack recipe through the existing held-use grinding handler and weak continuation,using human_herbing_grind.

Selected shard stacks debit one per commit. Stackable outputs use ordinary catalog merge/first-free insertion; individual existing outputs retain strict selected-slot replacement. ## FORGE OR-MIG-0019: Ancient and Forgotten brew drinking

## FORGE dependency correction: burnt-fish identities (OR-MIG-0037r2 / OR-MIG-0041r2)

While preparing member fish, exact Wiki variant pages exposed five incorrect ordinary-fish outputs in the published OR-MIG-0037 revision1. Corrected mappings are Sardine327 -> burnt369, Herring345 -> burnt357, and Trout335/Pike349/Salmon331 -> burnt343. Anchovies321 ->323, Tuna359 ->367, Lobster377 ->381 and Swordfish371 ->375 remain correct. The existing standard-range adapter inherits these corrected profiles.

The five common burnt-fish objects share the display name “Burnt fish”. The earlier focused test confirmed outputs against each recipe's own metadata; it did not establish semantic variant identity. New expected-ID assertions cover all nine profiles and exercise both the actual fire and standard-range configuration functions. Other prerequisite metadata in that configuration test is explicitly isolated; it is not fresh loaded-cache/native acceptance.

Pinned variant pages: Anchovies15188227 ->323, Sardine15188225 ->369, Herring15303865 ->357, Trout15290050 ->343 and Tuna15188231 ->367. The Bass6217775 redirect resolves to Tuna367. Herring includes mackerel; Trout includes cod/pike/salmon. These also correct the proposed member-fish role mapping before any member-fish implementation is published. Fresh correction preflight checked25ordinary F2Pdefinitions with explicit member flags. Cached burnt parameters were absent for the selected ordinary fish; absence was not treated as a numeric policy.

## FORGE OR-MIG-0044: member fish and fractional cooking XP

Registered nine member fish through the actual startup and existing item-on-fire/range cooking path: Mackerel353->355/357, Cod341->339/343, Bass363->365/367, Monkfish7944->7946/7948, Shark383->385/387, Sea turtle395->397/399, Manta ray389->391/393, Dark crab11934->11936/11938 and Anglerfish13439->13441/13443. The modular adapter is crates/server/src/content/member_fish.rs. It stages both method maps and member-input metadata before publication, checks exact canonical burnt IDs as well as names/ordinary flags, and uses the existing live membership gates, queue, RNG, inventory and Stats transaction.

Current Cooking requirements are10/18/43/62/80/82/91/90/84. Successful base XP is60/75/130/150/210/211.3/216.3/215/230; burns grant0XP. Added an optional base fine-XP override (tenths) to Recipe; all previous integer profiles use None and retain their values. Recipe::base_xp feeds the existing skills::advance function. There is no new XP engine or character schema. A present zero override is rejected before any item, Stats or RNG change. Cooking trace XP remains an integer; authoritative Stats.fine_xp and durable state must establish the fractional component.

Pinned Wiki rules distinguish Cod fire51/83-422 from range49/88-432 and Monkfish fire92/11-275 from range90/13-280. Mackerel uses45/98-452 and Bass80/33-312 on both methods. Shark, sea turtle, manta ray, dark crab and anglerfish have no forced cutoff and can burn at99 without the excluded modifiers; each method uses its pinned curve. Source rows truncate turtle/manta XP to211/216 and force several99cutoffs; those are deliberate Wiki adaptations. Monkfish cooking has no Swan Song quest requirement according to its pinned article; the fresh quest item7942 is not registered.

Range manual timing is1clock for Monkfish/Sea turtle/Manta ray and4for the other six. Their one-tick recipes list Cooking range as the facility. Fire remains at the observed donor default4for all nine; precise fire timing for those three is an explicit V4obligation, not an unqualified Wiki claim. Make-X and Cook-op1 are excluded.

Fresh corrected target preflight002 checked27ordinary definitions with explicit member flags. It preserves inspection001, which proposed wrong generic burnt aliases before the canonical variant pages were resolved. Mackerel357, Cod343 and Bass367 now have exact-ID guards, including a negative alias-permutation test. Six uniquely named burnt outputs also have pinned Wiki ID pages. No source expressions or assets were copied.

## FORGE OR-MIG-0047: shared random food healing and eight profiles

Extended the existing selected food transaction with optional inclusive RandomHealing, retaining eat/eat_slot/eat_slot_with_boosts APIs and all ordinary static/dynamic profiles. Actual queued Eat resolves the configured random effect after live membership and selected identity checks. Item debit/remainder and secondary-policy validation precede RNG; invalid range/negative boost, stale item, forbidden remainder, stack overflow or invalid remainder vars commit nothing and consume no RNG. Valid random food consumes at full HP/preserved overheal and still rolls its healing amount. Healing caps at live base HP and gives no XP. No new progression/healing engine, queue, global runtime or character schema.

Added exact ordinary member profiles: Thin snail3369 heals5–7, Lean snail3371 5–8, Fat snail3373 7–9, Spider stick6297 and shaft6299 7–10, Frog spawn5004 3–6, Cooked slimy eel3381 6–10 and Cave eel5003 8–12. Each uses existing food delay3 and human_eat829. Spider stick returns one stackable Skewer stick6305; shaft is destroyed. A skewer merges through the existing strict inventory insertion or occupies the consumed slot when no stack exists. Stack-return mode requires normal non-placeholder player inventory and rejects saturated/invalid-variable stacks atomically; no ground fallback is claimed. Existing nonstackable food remainders retain their strict selected-slot behavior. Runtime clears/rebuilds the random-effect map before admission. Selected traces record the consumed slot; merged returns must additionally be verified in the full inventory.

Pinned Wiki revisions: Thin15185782, Lean15185779, Fat15185778, Spider stick15184755/shaft15184756, Giant frogspawn redirect3742212 -> Frog spawn15326632, Slimy15184351, Cave15183749 and Skewer15197755. FoodSpecialEffectService uses the same inclusive ranges; FoodData provides row/item identities and static defaults, which are not substituted for the random effects. Source hashes and per-file inventory assessment recorded; independent behavior adaptation, no donor expressions/assets copied. The Wiki-specific stick return is explicit. Nine target definitions checked including stackable skewer and eight member Eat inputs; inspection001 and validated002 both preserved.
